        Idea:
# Sprint 3 — Step model overhaul, mutable plans, location-agnostic execution

Astrid reshape sprint 3. Full design: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/idea.md` § Sprint 3 (lines 214–295). This brief expands the sprint section for execution. **This is a large sprint and the architectural keystone of the reshape — premium/robust/high profile picked deliberately.**

Working directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid`. Start from branch `reshape/sprint-2`; create `reshape/sprint-3` off it and work there.

## Goal

Collapse the step model into one shape (no more `code`/`attested`/`nested` kinds). Make plans editable mid-run with explicit mutation rules. Make execution location-agnostic via three adapters (only `local` and `manual` ship this sprint; `remote-artifact` is reserved and rejected at runtime). Add the `claim` verb. Land the kernel-level pieces (validator, versioned step paths, mutation events, inbox path+version matching) that Sprint 4 + 5a all depend on.

**No orchestrator porting (Sprint 5a/5b territory).** Sprint 3 is the kernel + the adapters; existing packs are migrated forward mechanically.

## Foundational decisions (locked, do not re-debate)

### The new Step shape

```
Step {
  id: str
  version: int                       # bumped when a dispatched step is superseded
  adapter: "local" | "manual" | "remote-artifact"
  requires_ack: bool
  assignee: "system" | "agent:<id>" | "any-agent" | "human:<name>" | "any-human"
  produces: [{name, path, check}]
  repeat: until | for_each | none
  command: str?                      # leaf step: the thing to run; interpreted by adapter
  children: [Step]?                  # group step: nesting; replaces old `nested` kind
  cost: {amount, currency, source}?  # recorded at completion
  superseded_by: {
    to_version: int,
    scope: "all" | "future-iterations" | "future-items"  # REQUIRED; no default
  }?
}
```

- Leaf step has `command`, no `children`.
- Group step has `children`, no `command`. Its `produces` aggregates from descendants.
- `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are **gone** (migrated forward — see Migration).
- `version` starts at 1 for the initial step; bumps to 2+ on supersede.

### Supersession scope is mandatory

A `supersede-step` verb without an explicit `scope` is rejected at the verb. Three scopes:
- `"all"` — aborts in-flight iterations/items and restarts at iter/item 1 of the new version.
- `"future-iterations"` — `repeat.until` only; current iteration finishes against old version, iter+1 uses new version.
- `"future-items"` — `repeat.for_each` only; current item finishes against old version, next item uses new version.

### Plan mutation rules

- `plan.json` is the *initial* plan, written once at run start.
- The *effective* plan = `plan.json` + replayed mutation events from `events.jsonl`.
- **Append-only once dispatched.** A step is "dispatched" the moment `astrid next` returns its payload (event: `step_dispatched`). After dispatch:
  - `remove-step` is rejected. Use `abort` on the run instead, or `supersede-step`.
  - `edit-step` is rejected. Use `supersede-step` to bump version.
  - The original `steps/<id>/v1/` directory is **never deleted**.
- **Undispatched steps** can be added, edited, or removed freely. `remove-step` writes a tombstone event; the cursor skips tombstoned steps.
- **Canonical step path is always versioned**: every step lives at `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`. Iterations and items live under `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/` and `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`.

### The six plan-mutation invariants

Single function (call it `validate_mutation` or similar) runs **all six** invariants against the proposed effective tree:

1. **Schema validation** — full re-validate of the proposed effective tree (not a diff).
2. **Sibling-id uniqueness** at every frame (existing `plan.py:_assert_unique_paths` rule, applied to effective tree).
3. **`produces` reference integrity** — no dangling refs to deleted/tombstoned steps.
4. **Adapter validation** — declared adapter is `local | manual | remote-artifact`, command shape matches adapter (e.g. `manual` cannot have empty `command` string).
5. **Repeat-source validation** — `repeat.for_each.from_ref` resolves to a real prior `produces` in the effective tree.
6. **Lease epoch CAS** — the writer's epoch matches current (Sprint 1's lease).

Mutations that fail **any** check are rejected at the verb. No partial application. The verb returns a non-zero exit with the specific invariant that failed.

### Three adapters; only two ship this sprint

- **`local`** — `command` is a shell command run as a subprocess in the project root. Outputs land in `produces/`. Completion = exit code 0 + produces checks pass.
- **`manual`** — agent (or human) runs the work out-of-band; `command` is the dispatch payload (instructions). The agent is responsible for landing outputs in `produces/`. Completion = `astrid ack` (sync) or completion JSON in inbox (async).
- **`remote-artifact`** — schema-reserved here, **rejected at runtime** in Sprint 3 with: `astrid start / astrid next: step '<id>' declares adapter 'remote-artifact'; not yet implemented (Sprint 5a). Use --adapter local or manual.` Full implementation lands in Sprint 5a alongside the hype port.

Adapters are first-class code, not config.

### Ack identity at ack time, not author time

`astrid ack <step> --decision {approve,retry,iterate,abort} --agent <id>|--actor <name> [--evidence <path>]`. Both `--agent` and `--actor` are required (one or the other) — **no anonymous acks**. Every ack carries the `writer_epoch` it read; stale acks are rejected by the kernel.

### Authoring split

- **Leaf-template** — what we used to call an executor. One concrete unit of work. Same pack layout on disk (`pack/<name>/run.py` + `executor.yaml`).
- **Plan-template** — what we used to call an orchestrator: a reusable composition. Materializes into a step subtree at plan-build or plan-mutation time.

Runtime never sees templates — only the resulting step tree. During Sprint 3, the new template kinds land **alongside** the legacy `OrchestratorDefinition` YAML — both readable. Legacy YAML is materialized into the new step tree on the way in. (Sprint 5b removes legacy YAML acceptance.)

### Capture-everything rule

Anything producing an audit-worthy artifact = a step. The friction is small: `astrid plan add-step --command '...' --produces '...'` + `astrid next`. The alternative — agent runs python ad-hoc, artifact appears with no provenance — is what we're eliminating. Truly ephemeral commands don't need it.

## Deliverables

### Step schema + plan rewrite

- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/plan.py` against the new step shape. Keep `_assert_unique_paths` (or its equivalent in the new code) and the validators for the existing invariants; add what's needed for the new ones.
- Versioned step path everywhere: `steps/<id>/v<N>/...`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/iterations/NNN/`, `steps/<id>/v<N>/items/<item-id>/`. Update all path helpers in `astrid/core/task/`.
- **`code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds are gone.** Migration script (see below) rewrites existing plans.

### Plan-mutation verbs + event

- `astrid plan add-step` — insert a step into the effective tree at a given path. Position via `--after <step-path>` / `--before <step-path>` / `--into <group-path>`.
- `astrid plan edit-step` — modify an undispatched step (fields specifiable as flags).
- `astrid plan remove-step` — write a tombstone event for an undispatched step. Rejected on dispatched steps.
- `astrid plan supersede-step` — bump version on a dispatched (or any) step. **Requires `--scope {all,future-iterations,future-items}`.**
- All four verbs emit a single `plan_mutated` event in `events.jsonl` carrying: actor, writer_epoch, diff (the specific operation), timestamp. The hash chain extends to plan mutations.

### Plan-mutation validator

- Single entry point that runs all six invariants. Every verb routes through it. Rejection is total.
- Returns structured error: which invariant failed + the specific element that triggered it.
- Test: each invariant has at least one positive (passes) and one negative (rejects with the right error code) case.

### Cursor

- Cursor stores `(step_id, step_version, dispatch_event_hash)` per dispatched step.
- Walks the effective plan respecting tombstones and supersedes (honoring `scope` per repeat semantics).
- `astrid status` shows the cursor's current step at its current version.

### Inbox path+version match

- Rewrite `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` so entries match on `(plan_step_path, step_version, item_id?)` not just the leaf step id.
- Inbox entries from before a supersede event route to the original version's directory; entries after route to the new version.
- Stale entries that target a tombstoned or fully-superseded step move to `.rejected/` (existing pattern — extend it).

### Adapters

- `astrid/core/adapter/local.py` — subprocess-based local execution. Dispatch = spawn subprocess (sync or detached); completion = exit code 0 + produces checks; failure = non-zero exit OR missing produces.
- `astrid/core/adapter/manual.py` — emit dispatch payload (`command` field) to the agent's display path; completion = `astrid ack` event OR completion JSON arriving in inbox.
- `astrid/core/adapter/remote_artifact.py` — stub that rejects at dispatch time with the deferral message above.
- All three implement a common interface (e.g. `Adapter.dispatch(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.poll(step, run_ctx)` / `Adapter.complete(step, run_ctx)`). Pick the interface to fit how `astrid next` already works.

### Claim verb

- `astrid claim <step> [--for human:<name>] [--for agent:<id>]`. Default `--for` = current session's identity.
- Emits `claim` / `unclaim` events.
- Read-only sessions blocked from claiming.

### `requires_ack` enforcement

- Steps with `requires_ack: true` cannot complete on adapter completion alone; require an `astrid ack` event with a non-stale `writer_epoch`.
- `--agent` or `--actor` is **required** at ack time. Argparse rejects neither/both.

### Mutating verbs include `writer_epoch` + CAS

- Every mutating verb reads the current lease's `writer_epoch` before mutating; bumps it as part of the mutation; rejects if the epoch advanced under it.
- Relies on Sprint 1's lease primitives directly — do NOT reimplement.

### Cost field on completion events

- Each step completion event (`step_completed`, `step_failed`) carries `cost: {amount, currency, source}` if the adapter or executing code declared one.
- Adapters MAY emit cost; local subprocess execution typically won't. The field is optional but the schema reserves it (Sprint 4 + 5a depend on this).

### Inbox-driven async-completion documented + exercised

- Worked example for `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab. Demonstrate that closing the tab, opening a new one, `astrid attach`, `astrid next` picks up wherever inbox left things.
- Worked example for `manual` adapter: out-of-band ack landing as a completion JSON in inbox.
- Documentation lives at `docs/reshape/sprint-3/async-completion.md`.

### Migrations

- **Plan migration** — every existing `plan.json` rewritten to the collapsed schema. Every existing step gets `adapter: local` and `version: 1`. `code` / `attested` / `nested` step kinds rewritten to the new shape (best-effort mapping; ambiguous cases get `adapter: local` + a TODO note in the diff log).
- **Step path migration** — every existing `steps/<id>/...` directory becomes `steps/<id>/v1/...` in one shot. Run on `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/` (which is currently mostly empty — but the script must be idempotent and exercise the no-op path cleanly).
- Both migrations live under `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/`. `--dry-run` default; `--apply` commits.

### Hype port spike — back half of sprint, throwaway, do NOT merge to reshape/sprint-3

- Scratch branch `reshape/sprint-3-hype-spike` off `reshape/sprint-3`.
- Take the first three steps of `builtin.hype` only (transcribe → cut → render) and port them against the new step model.
- Deliverable is `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` enumerating every schema gap, adapter mismatch, and cost-field ambiguity discovered.
- **Schema revisions resulting from the spike land in Sprint 3, not deferred to a follow-up.** Revise the schema while it's still cheap.
- The spike branch is throwaway — push it for archival but do not merge.

### Tests

- `tests/task/test_plan_schema_collapsed.py` — new step schema validation, including all six invariants positive + negative.
- `tests/task/test_plan_mutation_verbs.py` — add/edit/remove/supersede each succeed in the happy path and reject correctly when post-dispatch / scope-missing / invariant-failing.
- `tests/task/test_step_versioning.py` — supersede produces `v2/`, `v1/` survives, cursor tracks correct version.
- `tests/task/test_cursor_supersede_scopes.py` — all three scopes exercised under repeat.until and repeat.for_each.
- `tests/task/test_inbox_path_version_match.py` — entries route correctly across a supersede event; stale entries land in `.rejected/`.
- `tests/adapter/test_local.py` — subprocess dispatch, completion, failure modes.
- `tests/adapter/test_manual.py` — dispatch payload emission, ack-driven completion, inbox-driven completion.
- `tests/adapter/test_remote_artifact_rejected.py` — `astrid next` rejects cleanly with the deferral message.
- `tests/task/test_claim_unclaim.py` — claim emits event, unclaim emits event, read-only session blocked.
- `tests/task/test_ack_identity_required.py` — argparse rejects no-agent-no-actor; both rejected too.
- `tests/migrations/test_plan_migration.py` — collapsed-schema rewrite across all old step kinds.
- `tests/migrations/test_step_path_migration.py` — `steps/<id>/...` → `steps/<id>/v1/...`, idempotence, no-op path.
- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still pass (regression — full pytest must come back green-modulo-known-pre-existing-failures).

## Stop-lines

1. **Hype spike reveals plan-template materialization can't express hype's dynamic discovery** (or any step shape is fundamentally wrong for hype). Halt before locking the Sprint 3 schema; redesign rather than push through. Write the redesign to `docs/reshape/sprint-3/schema-redesign.md` and stop for human review.
2. **Apex contract (S1) regresses under plan mutation events**. Locked event-append + epoch CAS is what plan-mutated rides on; if multi-tab races appear, halt and fix S1's primitives, not S3's surface.
3. **Inbox path+version routing loses entries silently**. If any inbox entry vanishes (not routed, not rejected, just gone) under any supersede or tombstone path, that's a stop-line — entries must always have a deterministic destination.

## Out of scope (do NOT touch)

- The `remote-artifact` adapter implementation (Sprint 5a).
- Orchestrator porting — hype proper, event_talks, thumbnail_maker (Sprint 5a/5b). The hype spike is a throwaway port of the first three steps only.
- RunPod lift (Sprint 4).
- Anything in `astrid/packs/builtin/human_review/`, `astrid/packs/seinfeld/` — pre-existing WIP, leave untouched.
- Export / verify / cost aggregation (Sprint 5b).
- `astrid projects timeline` (the legacy reigh-app subcommand) — leave it alone in this sprint; rename happens in Sprint 5b.

## Acceptance criteria

- `git branch --show-current` returns `reshape/sprint-3`.
- All Sprint 0 + Sprint 1 + Sprint 2 tests still green (modulo the 7 pre-existing unrelated failures verified in S2 — those are out of scope).
- `pytest tests/task/ tests/adapter/ tests/migrations/sprint-3/ -v` all green.
- Plan-mutation validator: every invariant has positive + negative test coverage.
- `astrid plan add-step / edit-step / remove-step / supersede-step` round-trip end-to-end on a freshly-created run.
- `astrid plan supersede-step` without `--scope` is rejected at argparse.
- `astrid plan edit-step` / `remove-step` on a dispatched step is rejected at the verb.
- `astrid plan remove-step` on an undispatched step writes a tombstone event; cursor skips it.
- `astrid claim` / `astrid unclaim` emit events; read-only session is blocked.
- `astrid ack` with neither `--agent` nor `--actor` is rejected at argparse.
- `local` adapter: subprocess that outlives the tab, reattach, `astrid next` picks up.
- `manual` adapter: dispatch + ack-driven completion + inbox-driven completion all work.
- `remote-artifact` adapter: rejected at `astrid next` with the deferral message.
- Hype spike: `docs/reshape/sprint-3/hype-spike-findings.md` exists with at least 3 enumerated gaps + the schema revisions that landed in response.
- Migrations: `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_plans.py --dry-run` and `--apply` both clean against `~/Documents/reigh-workspace/astrid-projects/`. `scripts/migrations/sprint-3/migrate_step_paths.py` likewise.

## Useful repo references

- `astrid/core/task/plan.py` — current step schema; will be rewritten.
- `astrid/core/task/events.py` — `append_event_locked` from Sprint 1; reuse for `plan_mutated`, `claim`, `unclaim`.
- `astrid/core/task/inbox.py` — current inbox; rewrite for path+version matching.
- `astrid/core/session/lease.py` — Sprint 1's lease with `writer_epoch`. CAS lives here; don't reimplement.
- `astrid/core/timeline/` — Sprint 2's package; do not modify in this sprint.
- `astrid/packs/builtin/hype/` — the spike's target; do NOT modify the real pack, only port to a scratch branch.
- `idea.md` § Sprint 3 (lines 214–295) — canonical sprint definition.
- `idea.md` § Foundation > Load-bearing decisions — the why behind these decisions.

## Style notes

- Type hints + dataclasses.
- Pathlib over os.path.
- Migration scripts: shebang, `chmod +x`, `--dry-run` default.
- All new modules include a one-line docstring.
- New tests follow existing test naming + structure conventions.
- For new event kinds (`plan_mutated`, `claim`, `unclaim`, `step_dispatched`): use `append_event_locked` from S1 — every emit is hash-chained and lease-CAS-protected.
- For supersede scope: model the three values as a string Literal (or enum), not a bare string. Argparse rejects unknown values.

## Megaplan-specific guidance

- This sprint is profile `premium/robust/high` because: (1) the kernel pieces here are downstream-cascading — every later sprint depends on these contracts; (2) the six-invariant validator is exactly the sort of contract that benefits from extra critique passes; (3) the hype spike loop has real architectural risk that wants high-depth planning.
- If the hype spike surfaces a stop-line, halt the sprint rather than push through. Document the gap and stop for human review.
- Use `astrid plan add-step` for dogfooding any sub-tasks of this sprint that are worth tracking — friction felt here is friction every user feels.

        Requirements:
        - If the engineering brief suggests an approach, use it as your starting hypothesis — but before committing, consider if there's a simpler or more fundamental fix. The brief is well-researched input, not a final answer.
        - If the brief is absent, incomplete, or says "skip", inspect the repository yourself before planning.
        - Stay focused on the requested idea. If repo exploration surfaces unrelated issues or docs, ignore them and return to the task.
        - Prefer source code, tests, and directly relevant config files. Avoid `.megaplan/`, prior plan artifacts, and unrelated `docs/` or ops/deployment material unless the task explicitly depends on them.
        - Stop exploring once you have enough evidence to name the concrete touch points and validation path. Do not keep browsing after you can write the plan.
        - Produce a concrete implementation plan in markdown.
        - Define observable success criteria as objects with `criterion` (string) and `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`):
          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Use the `questions` field for ambiguities that would materially change implementation.
        - Use the `assumptions` field for defaults you are making so planning can proceed now.
        - Prefer cheap validation steps early.
        - Keep the plan proportional to the task. A 1-line fix needs a 2-step plan (apply fix + run tests), not a 5-step investigation.
        - If user notes answer earlier questions, incorporate them into the draft plan instead of re-asking them.
        - Fix the problem fully. Do not limit scope just to avoid breaking existing tests — update the tests too if needed.
        - Prefer the simplest, most direct fix. No fallbacks, type conversions, or defensive wrappers without concrete evidence they are needed.
        - If the task or issue hints suggest a specific approach, follow it. Only deviate with concrete counter-evidence.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

115	def step_dir_for_path(
116	    slug: str,
117	    run_id: str,
118	    plan_step_path: tuple[str, ...],
119	    *,
120	    iteration: int | None = None,

124	    validate_project_slug(slug)
125	    validate_run_id(run_id)
126	    if not plan_step_path:
127	        raise TaskPlanError("plan_step_path must contain at least one segment")
128	    for segment in plan_step_path:
129	        validate_run_id(segment)
130	    if iteration is not None and item_id is not None:
131	        raise TaskPlanError("step_dir_for_path: iteration and item_id are mutually exclusive")
132	    base = project_dir(slug, root=root) / "runs" / run_id / "steps"
133	    for segment in plan_step_path:
134	        base = base / segment
135	    if iteration is not None:
136	        if not isinstance(iteration, int) or isinstance(iteration, bool) or iteration < 1:
137	            raise TaskPlanError("step_dir_for_path: iteration must be an int >= 1")
138	        base = base / "iterations" / f"{iteration:03d}"
139	    elif item_id is not None:
140	        validate_run_id(item_id)
141	        base = base / "items" / item_id
142	    return base

155	def iter_steps_with_path(plan: TaskPlan) -> Iterator[tuple[tuple[str, ...], TaskPlanStep]]:
156	    """Yield ``(path, step)`` in pre-order for every step in the plan tree."""
157	
158	    def _walk(steps: tuple[TaskPlanStep, ...], prefix: tuple[str, ...]) -> Iterator[tuple[tuple[str, ...], TaskPlanStep]]:
159	        for step in steps:
160	            path = prefix + (step.id,)
161	            yield path, step
162	            if isinstance(step, NestedStep):
163	                yield from _walk(step.plan.steps, path)
164	
165	    yield from _walk(plan.steps, ())

168	def parse_from_ref(from_ref: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
169	    """Parse '<step-id>.produces.<name>' into (step_id, produces_name)."""
170	    sep = ".produces."
171	    idx = from_ref.find(sep)
172	    if idx <= 0 or idx + len(sep) >= len(from_ref):
173	        raise TaskPlanError(
174	            f"repeat.for_each.from must match '<step-id>.produces.<name>', got {from_ref!r}"
175	        )
176	    return from_ref[:idx], from_ref[idx + len(sep):]
177

179	def _step_to_dict(step: TaskPlanStep) -> dict[str, Any]:
180	    if isinstance(step, CodeStep):
181	        out: dict[str, Any] = {"id": step.id, "kind": "code", "command": step.command}
182	    elif isinstance(step, AttestedStep):
183	        out = {
184	            "id": step.id,
185	            "kind": "attested",
186	            "command": step.command,
187	            "instructions": step.instructions,
188	            "ack": {"kind": step.ack.kind},
189	        }
190	    elif isinstance(step, NestedStep):
191	        out = {
192	            "id": step.id,
193	            "kind": "nested",
194	            "plan": step.plan.to_dict(),
195	        }
196	    else:
197	        raise TaskPlanError(f"unknown step type: {type(step)!r}")
198	    if step.produces:
199	        out["produces"] = _produces_to_dict(step.produces)
200	    if step.repeat is not None:
201	        out["repeat"] = _repeat_to_dict(step.repeat)
202	    return out

205	def _produces_to_dict(produces: tuple[ProducesEntry, ...]) -> dict[str, Any]:
206	    sorted_entries = sorted(produces, key=lambda entry: entry.name)
207	    return {
208	        entry.name: {
209	            "path": entry.path,
210	            "check": {
211	                "check_id": entry.check.check_id,
212	                "params": canonical_check_params(entry.check.params),
213	                "sentinel": entry.check.sentinel,
214	            },
215	        }
216	        for entry in sorted_entries
217	    }
218	
219	
220	def _repeat_to_dict(repeat: Repeat) -> dict[str, Any]:
221	    if isinstance(repeat, RepeatUntil):
222	        out: dict[str, Any] = {
223	            "until": repeat.condition,
224	            "max_iterations": repeat.max_iterations,
225	            "on_exhaust": repeat.on_exhaust,
226	        }
227	        if repeat.quorum_n is not None:
228	            out["quorum_n"] = repeat.quorum_n
229	        return out
230	    if isinstance(repeat, RepeatForEach):
231	        if repeat.items_source == "static":
232	            return {"for_each": {"items": list(repeat.items)}}
233	        return {"for_each": {"from": repeat.from_ref}}
234	    raise TaskPlanError(f"unknown repeat type: {type(repeat)!r}")
235	
236	
237	def _read_plan_payload(plan_path: str | Path) -> Any:
238	    path = Path(plan_path)
239	    try:
240	        return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
241	    except FileNotFoundError:
242	        raise
243	    except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
244	        raise TaskPlanError(f"invalid JSON in {path}: {exc.msg}") from exc
245	    except OSError as exc:
246	        raise TaskPlanError(f"failed to read {path}: {exc}") from exc
247	
248	
249	def _validate_plan(payload: Any, *, _is_root: bool = True) -> TaskPlan:
250	    if not isinstance(payload, dict):
251	        raise TaskPlanError("plan must be an object")
252	    plan_id = payload.get("plan_id")
253	    version = payload.get("version")
254	    steps = payload.get("steps")
255	    if not isinstance(plan_id, str) or not plan_id:
256	        raise TaskPlanError("plan plan_id must be a non-empty string")
257	    if version != 1 or isinstance(version, bool):
258	        raise TaskPlanError("plan version must be 1")
259	    if not isinstance(steps, list):
260	        raise TaskPlanError("plan steps must be a list")
261	
262	    validated_steps: list[TaskPlanStep] = []
263	    for index, step in enumerate(steps):
264	        validated_steps.append(_validate_step(step, index, validated_steps))
265	
266	    plan = TaskPlan(plan_id=plan_id, version=1, steps=tuple(validated_steps))
267	    if _is_root:
268	        _assert_unique_paths(plan)
269	    return plan
270	
271	
272	def _validate_step(step: Any, index: int, prior_siblings: list[TaskPlanStep]) -> TaskPlanStep:
273	    if not isinstance(step, dict):
274	        raise TaskPlanError(f"plan steps[{index}] must be an object")
275	    kind = step.get("kind", "code")
276	    if kind == "code":
277	        return _validate_code_step(step, index, prior_siblings)
278	    if kind == "attested":
279	        return _validate_attested_step(step, index, prior_siblings)
280	    if kind == "nested":
281	        return _validate_nested_step(step, index, prior_siblings)
282	    raise TaskPlanError(
283	        f"plan steps[{index}].kind must be one of {TASK_STEP_KINDS}, got {kind!r}"
284	    )
285	
286	
287	def _validate_code_step(step: dict[str, Any], index: int, prior_siblings: list[TaskPlanStep]) -> CodeStep:
288	    step_id = step.get("id")
289	    command = step.get("command")
290	    if not isinstance(step_id, str) or not step_id:
291	        raise TaskPlanError(f"plan steps[{index}].id must be a non-empty string")
292	    if not isinstance(command, str) or not command:
293	        raise TaskPlanError(f"plan steps[{index}].command must be a non-empty string")
294	    _reject_orchestrators_run(command, index)
295	    produces = _validate_produces(step.get("produces"), index, allow_legacy_list=True)
296	    repeat = _validate_repeat(step.get("repeat"), index, prior_siblings)
297	    return CodeStep(
298	        id=validate_run_id(step_id),
299	        command=command,
300	        produces=produces,
301	        repeat=repeat,
302	    )
303	
304	
305	def _validate_attested_step(step: dict[str, Any], index: int, prior_siblings: list[TaskPlanStep]) -> AttestedStep:
306	    step_id = step.get("id")
307	    command = step.get("command")
308	    instructions = step.get("instructions")
309	    raw_produces = step.get("produces")
310	    ack = step.get("ack")
311	    if not isinstance(step_id, str) or not step_id:
312	        raise TaskPlanError(f"plan steps[{index}].id must be a non-empty string")
313	    if not isinstance(command, str) or not command:
314	        raise TaskPlanError(f"plan steps[{index}].command must be a non-empty string")
315	    if not isinstance(instructions, str) or not instructions:
316	        raise TaskPlanError(
317	            f"plan steps[{index}].instructions must be a non-empty string for attested step"
318	        )
319	    if isinstance(raw_produces, list) and raw_produces:
320	        raise TaskPlanError(
321	            f"plan steps[{index}].produces is a sentinel-only list; attested produces require a semantic check (use dict form with non-sentinel check)"
322	        )
323	    produces = _validate_produces(raw_produces, index, allow_legacy_list=False)
324	    for entry in produces:
325	        if entry.check.sentinel:
326	            raise TaskPlanError(
327	                f"plan steps[{index}].produces[{entry.name!r}] uses sentinel-only check {entry.check.check_id!r}; attested produces requires a semantic check"
328	            )
329	    if not isinstance(ack, dict):
330	        raise TaskPlanError(f"plan steps[{index}].ack must be an object")
331	    ack_kind = ack.get("kind")
332	    if ack_kind not in {"agent", "actor"}:
333	        raise TaskPlanError(
334	            f"plan steps[{index}].ack.kind must be 'agent' or 'actor', got {ack_kind!r}"
335	        )
336	    repeat = _validate_repeat(step.get("repeat"), index, prior_siblings)
337	    return AttestedStep(
338	        id=validate_run_id(step_id),
339	        command=command,
340	        instructions=instructions,
341	        produces=produces,
342	        ack=AckRule(kind=ack_kind),
343	        repeat=repeat,
344	    )
345	
346	
347	def _validate_nested_step(step: dict[str, Any], index: int, prior_siblings: list[TaskPlanStep]) -> NestedStep:
348	    step_id = step.get("id")
349	    child_plan = step.get("plan")
350	    if not isinstance(step_id, str) or not step_id:
351	        raise TaskPlanError(f"plan steps[{index}].id must be a non-empty string")
352	    if not isinstance(child_plan, dict):
353	        raise TaskPlanError(
354	            f"plan steps[{index}].plan must be a nested plan object"
355	        )
356	    nested = _validate_plan(child_plan, _is_root=False)
357	    produces = _validate_produces(step.get("produces"), index, allow_legacy_list=False)
358	    repeat = _validate_repeat(step.get("repeat"), index, prior_siblings)
359	    return NestedStep(
360	        id=validate_run_id(step_id),
361	        plan=nested,
362	        produces=produces,
363	        repeat=repeat,
364	    )
365	
366	
367	def _validate_produces(raw: Any, index: int, *, allow_legacy_list: bool) -> tuple[ProducesEntry, ...]:
368	    if raw is None:
369	        return ()
370	    if isinstance(raw, list):
371	        if not allow_legacy_list:
372	            raise TaskPlanError(
373	                f"plan steps[{index}].produces must be a dict; legacy list form not allowed for this step kind"
374	            )
375	        if not raw:
376	            return ()
377	        entries: list[ProducesEntry] = []
378	        seen: set[str] = set()
379	        for item in raw:
380	            if not isinstance(item, str) or not item:
381	                raise TaskPlanError(
382	                    f"plan steps[{index}].produces legacy list must contain non-empty strings"
383	                )
384	            name = Path(item).stem or item
385	            if name in seen:
386	                raise TaskPlanError(
387	                    f"plan steps[{index}].produces legacy list yields duplicate name {name!r}; use dict form"
388	                )
389	            seen.add(name)
390	            entries.append(ProducesEntry(name=name, path=item, check=file_nonempty()))
391	        return tuple(entries)
392	    if isinstance(raw, dict):
393	        entries = []
394	        for name in raw:
395	            if not isinstance(name, str) or not name:
396	                raise TaskPlanError(
397	                    f"plan steps[{index}].produces keys must be non-empty strings"
398	                )
399	            value = raw[name]
400	            if not isinstance(value, dict):
401	                raise TaskPlanError(
402	                    f"plan steps[{index}].produces[{name!r}] must be an object"
403	                )
404	            path_value = value.get("path")
405	            if not isinstance(path_value, str) or not path_value:
406	                raise TaskPlanError(
407	                    f"plan steps[{index}].produces[{name!r}].path must be a non-empty string"
408	                )
409	            check = _validate_check(value.get("check"), index, name)
410	            entries.append(ProducesEntry(name=name, path=path_value, check=check))
411	        return tuple(entries)
412	    raise TaskPlanError(
413	        f"plan steps[{index}].produces must be a dict (or legacy list for code steps)"
414	    )
415	
416	
417	def _validate_check(raw: Any, index: int, produces_name: str) -> Check:
418	    if not isinstance(raw, dict):
419	        raise TaskPlanError(
420	            f"plan steps[{index}].produces[{produces_name!r}].check must be an object"
421	        )
422	    check_id = raw.get("check_id")
423	    if not isinstance(check_id, str) or not check_id:
424	        raise TaskPlanError(
425	            f"plan steps[{index}].produces[{produces_name!r}].check.check_id must be a non-empty string"
426	        )
427	    params = raw.get("params", {})
428	    if not isinstance(params, dict):
429	        raise TaskPlanError(
430	            f"plan steps[{index}].produces[{produces_name!r}].check.params must be an object"
431	        )
432	    sentinel = raw.get("sentinel", False)
433	    if not isinstance(sentinel, bool):
434	        raise TaskPlanError(
435	            f"plan steps[{index}].produces[{produces_name!r}].check.sentinel must be a bool"
436	        )
437	    return Check(check_id=check_id, params=canonical_check_params(params), sentinel=sentinel)
438	
439	
440	def _validate_repeat(raw: Any, index: int, prior_siblings: list[TaskPlanStep]) -> Repeat | None:
441	    if raw is None:
442	        return None
443	    if not isinstance(raw, dict):
444	        raise TaskPlanError(f"plan steps[{index}].repeat must be an object")
445	    has_until = "until" in raw
446	    has_for_each = "for_each" in raw
447	    if has_until and has_for_each:
448	        raise TaskPlanError(
449	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat cannot have both 'until' and 'for_each'"
450	        )
451	    if not has_until and not has_for_each:
452	        raise TaskPlanError(
453	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat must contain 'until' or 'for_each'"
454	        )
455	    if has_until:
456	        return _validate_repeat_until(raw, index)
457	    return _validate_repeat_for_each(raw["for_each"], index, prior_siblings)
458	
459	
460	def _validate_repeat_until(raw: dict[str, Any], index: int) -> RepeatUntil:
461	    condition = raw.get("until")
462	    if condition not in {"user_approves", "verifier_passes", "quorum"}:
463	        raise TaskPlanError(
464	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.until must be one of 'user_approves','verifier_passes','quorum', got {condition!r}"
465	        )
466	    max_iterations = raw.get("max_iterations")
467	    if not isinstance(max_iterations, int) or isinstance(max_iterations, bool) or max_iterations < 1:
468	        raise TaskPlanError(
469	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.max_iterations must be an int >= 1"
470	        )
471	    on_exhaust = raw.get("on_exhaust")
472	    if on_exhaust not in {"escalate", "fail"}:
473	        raise TaskPlanError(
474	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.on_exhaust must be 'escalate' or 'fail', got {on_exhaust!r}"
475	        )
476	    quorum_n = raw.get("quorum_n")
477	    if condition == "quorum":
478	        if not isinstance(quorum_n, int) or isinstance(quorum_n, bool) or quorum_n < 1:
479	            raise TaskPlanError(
480	                f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.quorum_n must be an int >= 1 when until='quorum'"
481	            )
482	    elif quorum_n is not None:
483	        raise TaskPlanError(
484	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.quorum_n only valid when until='quorum'"
485	        )
486	    return RepeatUntil(
487	        condition=condition,
488	        max_iterations=max_iterations,
489	        on_exhaust=on_exhaust,
490	        quorum_n=quorum_n,
491	    )
492	
493	
494	def _validate_repeat_for_each(raw: Any, index: int, prior_siblings: list[TaskPlanStep]) -> RepeatForEach:
495	    if not isinstance(raw, dict):
496	        raise TaskPlanError(
497	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.for_each must be an object"
498	        )
499	    has_items = "items" in raw
500	    has_from = "from" in raw
501	    if has_items == has_from:
502	        raise TaskPlanError(
503	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.for_each must have exactly one of 'items' or 'from'"
504	        )
505	    if has_items:
506	        items = raw["items"]
507	        if not isinstance(items, list) or not all(isinstance(x, str) and x for x in items):
508	            raise TaskPlanError(
509	                f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.for_each.items must be a list of non-empty strings"
510	            )
511	        return RepeatForEach(items_source="static", items=tuple(items), from_ref=None)
512	    from_ref = raw["from"]
513	    if not isinstance(from_ref, str) or not from_ref:
514	        raise TaskPlanError(
515	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.for_each.from must be a non-empty string"
516	        )
517	    target_id, produces_name = parse_from_ref(from_ref)
518	    target = next((s for s in prior_siblings if s.id == target_id), None)
519	    if target is None:
520	        raise TaskPlanError(
521	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.for_each.from references unknown prior sibling step {target_id!r}"
522	        )
523	    if not any(p.name == produces_name for p in target.produces):
524	        raise TaskPlanError(
525	            f"plan steps[{index}].repeat.for_each.from references unknown produces {produces_name!r} on step {target_id!r}"
526	        )
527	    return RepeatForEach(items_source="from", items=(), from_ref=from_ref)
528	
529	
530	def _reject_orchestrators_run(command: str, index: int) -> None:
531	    try:
532	        tokens = shlex.split(command)
533	    except ValueError as exc:
534	        raise TaskPlanError(
535	            f"plan steps[{index}].command is not shell-parseable: {exc}"
536	        ) from exc
537	    tokens = _strip_astrid_prefix(tokens)
538	    if len(tokens) >= 2 and tokens[0] == "orchestrators" and tokens[1] == "run":
539	        raise TaskPlanError(
540	            "code step argv targets 'astrid orchestrators run'; use a nested step"
541	        )
542	
543	
544	def _strip_astrid_prefix(tokens: list[str]) -> list[str]:
545	    if (
546	        len(tokens) >= 3
547	        and Path(tokens[0]).name.startswith("python")
548	        and tokens[1:3] == ["-m", "astrid"]
549	    ):
550	        return tokens[3:]
551	    if tokens and Path(tokens[0]).name.endswith("astrid"):
552	        return tokens[1:]
553	    return tokens
554	
555	
556	def _assert_unique_paths(plan: TaskPlan) -> None:
557	    """Reject sibling-id collisions inside any one frame.
558	
559	    Identical leaf ids in different subtrees are accepted because their full paths differ.
560	    """
561	
562	    def _check(steps: tuple[TaskPlanStep, ...], prefix: tuple[str, ...]) -> None:
563	        seen: set[str] = set()
564	        for step in steps:
565	            if step.id in seen:
566	                location = STEP_PATH_SEP.join(prefix + (step.id,))
567	                raise TaskPlanError(
568	                    f"duplicate step id {step.id!r} among siblings at {location!r}"
569	                )
570	            seen.add(step.id)
571	            if isinstance(step, NestedStep):
572	                _check(step.plan.steps, prefix + (step.id,))
573	
574	    _check(plan.steps, ())
575

1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""Astrid top-level command gateway.
3	
4	Sprint 1 wires the session CLI gate: every verb outside the unbound
5	allowlist requires ``ASTRID_SESSION_ID`` to resolve to a valid session
6	record. Unbound callers are pointed at ``astrid attach <project>``.
7	
8	The unbound allowlist mirrors the brief: ``attach``, ``status``,
9	``projects ls``, ``projects create``, ``sessions ls``,
10	``sessions takeover``, ``sessions detach``, ``init`` (first-run
11	bootstrap), and the help flags. ``author test --project <slug>`` is also
12	a documented exception so workflow tests can run without an operator
13	session.
14	
15	Subcommands dispatch to focused module CLIs. Brief / video flags fall
16	through to the ``builtin.hype`` orchestrator resolved through the
17	orchestrator registry.
18	"""
19	
20	from __future__ import annotations
21	
22	import sys
23	from typing import Iterable
24	
25	
26	# Phase 5 lifecycle verbs short-circuit the implicit task-mode gate at the top
27	# of main(): for these verbs the --project flag identifies the run, NOT a
28	# command to dispatch through plan[cursor]. cmd_ack approve re-enters the gate
29	# explicitly (see lifecycle_ack._ack_approve), so the short-circuit only
30	# bypasses the gate's command-match step.
31	LIFECYCLE_VERBS = {"start", "next", "ack", "abort", "status", "runs", "hook"}
32	
33	
34	# Sprint 1 session-gate allowlist. A first-token (or two-token) match against
35	# this set lets the verb run without a bound session. Everything else needs
36	# ``ASTRID_SESSION_ID`` to resolve to a session record.
37	_UNBOUND_TOP_LEVEL = {
38	    "attach",
39	    "status",
40	    "sessions",  # sub-verbs handled below
41	    "init",
42	    "-h",
43	    "--help",
44	}
45	_UNBOUND_PROJECTS_SUBVERBS = {"ls", "create"}
46	_UNBOUND_SESSIONS_SUBVERBS = {"ls", "takeover", "detach"}
47	
48	
49	def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
50	    raw = sys.argv[1:] if argv is None else list(argv)
51	    if raw and raw[0] in {"-h", "--help"}:
52	        _print_entrypoint_help()
53	        return 0
54	    # Nudge runs once per CLI invocation, before the command itself, but never
55	    # for the `skills` subcommand (would be silly) or help. Cheap state-file
56	    # read; bails early if no harness is detected or ARTAGENTS_NO_NUDGE is set.
57	    try:
58	        from .skills import nudge_if_needed
59	
60	        nudge_if_needed(argv=raw)
61	    except Exception:
62	        # Never let the nudge break a real command.
63	        pass
64	
65	    # Session gate. Verbs outside the unbound allowlist require a resolvable
66	    # session record; print the documented hint and exit 2 otherwise.
67	    if not _verb_is_unbound_allowlisted(raw):
68	        from .core.session.binding import (
69	            ASTRID_SESSION_ID_ENV,  # noqa: F401 — referenced in the error path
70	            SessionBindingError,
71	            resolve_current_session,
72	        )
73	
74	        try:
75	            session = resolve_current_session()
76	        except SessionBindingError as exc:
77	            print(f"session: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
78	            return 2
79	        if session is None:
80	            print(
81	                "no session bound — run `astrid attach <project>`",
82	                file=sys.stderr,
83	            )
84	            return 2
85	
86	    if raw and raw[0] in LIFECYCLE_VERBS:
87	        return _dispatch(raw)
88	    project_slug = _extract_project_slug(raw)
89	    if project_slug is None:
90	        return _dispatch(raw)
91	
92	    from .core.task import gate as task_gate
93	
94	    try:
95	        decision = task_gate.gate_command(project_slug, task_gate.command_for_argv(raw), raw)
96	    except task_gate.TaskRunGateError as exc:
97	        print(f"task-mode gate rejected: {exc.reason}\nrecovery: {exc.recovery}", file=sys.stderr)
98	        return 1
99	    if not decision.active:
100	        return _dispatch(raw)
101	
102	    returncode = -1
103	    try:
104	        returncode = _dispatch(raw)
105	        return returncode
106	    finally:
107	        # T9 extends GateDecision with a `.session` field so the post-dispatch
108	        # record helpers can flow through a fresh WriterContext. Until that
109	        # lands, guard the wrapper with hasattr() so existing callers that
110	        # don't carry a session keep working unchanged.
111	        if hasattr(decision, "session") and getattr(decision, "session", None) is not None:
112	            from .core.session.writer import writer_context_from_decision
113	
114	            try:
115	                with writer_context_from_decision(decision):
116	                    task_gate.record_dispatch_complete(decision, returncode)
117	            except Exception:
118	                # Fall back to the unwrapped path on any writer-auth failure;
119	                # T9's lifecycle migration is the layer that makes this hard.
120	                task_gate.record_dispatch_complete(decision, returncode)
121	        else:
122	            task_gate.record_dispatch_complete(decision, returncode)
123	
124	
125	def _verb_is_unbound_allowlisted(raw: list[str]) -> bool:
126	    """Decide whether the invocation may run without a bound session.
127	
128	    The allowlist is the canonical Sprint 1 set (brief §CLI gate):
129	
130	    * ``attach``, ``init``, ``-h`` / ``--help`` (full-verb).
131	    * ``status`` — both the new session breadcrumb (no ``--project``) and
132	      the legacy ``astrid status --project <slug>``.
133	    * ``projects ls`` and ``projects create``.
134	    * ``sessions ls`` / ``sessions takeover`` / ``sessions detach``.
135	    * ``author test --project <slug>`` — documented exception for the
136	      workflow test runner.
137	    """
138	
139	    if not raw:
140	        return True  # empty argv → entrypoint help
141	
142	    top = raw[0]
143	    if top in {"-h", "--help"}:
144	        return True
145	    if top in {"attach", "init", "status"}:
146	        return True
147	    if top == "projects" and len(raw) >= 2 and raw[1] in _UNBOUND_PROJECTS_SUBVERBS:
148	        return True
149	    if top == "timelines" and len(raw) >= 2 and raw[1] == "ls":
150	        return True
151	    if top == "sessions" and len(raw) >= 2 and raw[1] in _UNBOUND_SESSIONS_SUBVERBS:
152	        return True
153	    # `author test --project <slug>` exception. The orchestrate.cli wires the
154	    # `test` sub-verb regardless of whether a session is bound, so we open the
155	    # gate explicitly to match.
156	    if top == "author" and "test" in raw[1:] and "--project" in raw:
157	        return True
158	    return False
159	
160	
161	def _dispatch(raw: list[str]) -> int:
162	    if raw and raw[0] == "attach":
163	        from .core.session.cli import build_parser as _sb
164	        from .core.session.cli import cmd_attach
165	
166	        args = _sb().parse_args(["attach", *raw[1:]])
167	        return int(cmd_attach(args))
168	    if raw and raw[0] == "sessions":
169	        return _dispatch_sessions(raw[1:])
170	    if raw and raw[0] == "start":
171	        from .core.task.lifecycle import cmd_start
172	
173	        return cmd_start(raw[1:])
174	    if raw and raw[0] == "next":
175	        from .core.task.lifecycle import cmd_next
176	
177	        return cmd_next(raw[1:])
178	    if raw and raw[0] == "ack":
179	        from .core.task.lifecycle import cmd_ack
180	
181	        return cmd_ack(raw[1:])
182	    if raw and raw[0] == "abort":
183	        from .core.task.lifecycle import cmd_abort
184	
185	        return cmd_abort(raw[1:])
186	    if raw and raw[0] == "status":
187	        # The new session-status verb fires when no --project is given; the
188	        # legacy lifecycle status verb keeps working with --project.
189	        if "--project" in raw[1:]:
190	            from .core.task.lifecycle import cmd_status
191	
192	            return cmd_status(raw[1:])
193	        from .core.session.cli import build_parser as _sb
194	        from .core.session.cli import cmd_status as session_status
195	
196	        args = _sb().parse_args(["status"])
197	        return int(session_status(args))
198	    if raw and raw[0] == "runs":
199	        return _dispatch_runs(raw[1:])

269	def _dispatch_sessions(args: list[str]) -> int:
270	    if not args:
271	        print(
272	            "usage: astrid sessions {ls,detach,takeover} ...",
273	            file=sys.stderr,
274	        )
275	        return 2
276	    from .core.session.cli import (
277	        build_parser,
278	        cmd_sessions_detach,
279	        cmd_sessions_ls,
280	        cmd_sessions_takeover,
281	    )
282	
283	    sub = args[0]
284	    parser = build_parser()
285	    if sub == "ls":
286	        parsed = parser.parse_args(["ls"])
287	        return int(cmd_sessions_ls(parsed))
288	    if sub == "detach":
289	        parsed = parser.parse_args(["detach", *args[1:]])
290	        return int(cmd_sessions_detach(parsed))
291	    if sub == "takeover":
292	        parsed = parser.parse_args(["takeover", *args[1:]])
293	        return int(cmd_sessions_takeover(parsed))
294	    print(
295	        f"sessions: unknown sub-verb {sub!r}; expected one of ls / detach / takeover",
296	        file=sys.stderr,
297	    )
298	    return 2

304	        return 2

326	def _extract_project_slug(raw: list[str]) -> str | None:
327	    for index, token in enumerate(raw):
328	        if token == "--project":
329	            return raw[index + 1] if index + 1 < len(raw) else None
330	        if token.startswith("--project="):
331	            value = token.split("=", 1)[1]
332	            return value or None
333	    return None

336	def _run_default_brief_orchestrator(argv: list[str]) -> int:
337	    from importlib import import_module
338	
339	    from .core.orchestrator.registry import load_default_registry
340	
341	    registry = load_default_registry()
342	    orchestrator = registry.get("builtin.hype")
343	    runtime_module = orchestrator.metadata.get("runtime_module")
344	    runtime_entrypoint = orchestrator.metadata.get("runtime_entrypoint", "main")
345	    if not isinstance(runtime_module, str) or not runtime_module:
346	        raise RuntimeError("builtin.hype manifest is missing metadata.runtime_module")
347	    module = import_module(runtime_module)
348	    entrypoint = getattr(module, runtime_entrypoint)
349	    return int(entrypoint(argv))

1	"""Inbox surface for external completion signals (Phase 8).
2	
3	External processes drop JSON files into ``runs/<run-id>/inbox/`` to signal
4	that an attested step has completed. ``astrid next`` consumes these
5	entries before computing the next step.
6	"""
7	
8	from __future__ import annotations
9	
10	import hashlib
11	import json
12	import logging
13	import os
14	import shlex
15	from dataclasses import dataclass
16	from pathlib import Path
17	
18	from astrid.core.project.paths import project_dir

21	    append_event,

24	)

39	
40	INBOX_DIR_NAME = "inbox"
41	CONSUMED_DIR_NAME = ".consumed"
42	REJECTED_DIR_NAME = ".rejected"
43	
44	_VALID_DECISIONS = ("approve", "retry", "abort")
45	
46	_LOGGER = logging.getLogger("astrid.core.task.inbox")
47	
48	
49	class InboxValidationError(Exception):
50	    """Raised internally when an inbox file fails schema validation."""
51	
52	
53	@dataclass(frozen=True)
54	class InboxEntry:
55	    path: Path
56	    step_id: str
57	    decision: str
58	    evidence: tuple[str, ...]
59	    submitted_at: str
60	    submitted_by: str
61	    item_id: str | None
62	    raw: dict
63	
64	
65	def inbox_dir(run_dir: Path) -> Path:
66	    return run_dir / INBOX_DIR_NAME
67	
68	
69	def _parse_entry(file_path: Path, raw: dict) -> InboxEntry:
70	    if not isinstance(raw, dict):
71	        raise InboxValidationError("payload must be a JSON object")
72	
73	    step_id = raw.get("step_id")
74	    if not isinstance(step_id, str) or not step_id:
75	        raise InboxValidationError("missing or empty step_id")
76	
77	    decision = raw.get("decision")
78	    if decision not in _VALID_DECISIONS:
79	        raise InboxValidationError(
80	            f"decision must be one of {_VALID_DECISIONS}, got {decision!r}"
81	        )
82	
83	    submitted_at = raw.get("submitted_at")
84	    if not isinstance(submitted_at, str):
85	        raise InboxValidationError("submitted_at must be a string")
86	
87	    submitted_by = raw.get("submitted_by")
88	    if not isinstance(submitted_by, str):
89	        raise InboxValidationError("submitted_by must be a string")
90	
91	    evidence_raw = raw.get("evidence")
92	    if evidence_raw is None:
93	        evidence: tuple[str, ...] = ()
94	    else:
95	        if not isinstance(evidence_raw, dict):
96	            raise InboxValidationError("evidence must be a JSON object")
97	        evidence_values: list[str] = []
98	        for key, value in evidence_raw.items():
99	            if not isinstance(value, str) or not value:
100	                raise InboxValidationError(
101	                    f"evidence value for {key!r} must be a non-empty string"
102	                )
103	            evidence_values.append(value)
104	        evidence = tuple(evidence_values)
105	
106	    item_id_raw = raw.get("item_id")
107	    if item_id_raw is None:
108	        item_id: str | None = None
109	    elif isinstance(item_id_raw, str) and item_id_raw:
110	        item_id = item_id_raw
111	    else:
112	        raise InboxValidationError("item_id must be a non-empty string when present")
113	
114	    return InboxEntry(
115	        path=file_path,
116	        step_id=step_id,
117	        decision=decision,
118	        evidence=evidence,
119	        submitted_at=submitted_at,
120	        submitted_by=submitted_by,
121	        item_id=item_id,
122	        raw=raw,
123	    )
124	
125	
126	def scan_inbox(run_dir: Path) -> list[InboxEntry]:
127	    """Read and validate inbox entries.
128	
129	    Returns ``[]`` when the inbox directory is absent (opt-in behavior).
130	    Subdirectories and dot-prefixed names are skipped. Malformed files are
131	    logged via ``_LOGGER.warning`` and skipped — never raised.
132	    Entries are sorted by ``(submitted_at, filename)`` for deterministic ordering.
133	    """
134	    directory = inbox_dir(run_dir)
135	    if not directory.is_dir():
136	        return []
137	
138	    entries: list[InboxEntry] = []
139	    for child in directory.iterdir():
140	        if child.name.startswith("."):
141	            continue
142	        if not child.is_file():
143	            continue
144	        try:
145	            data = child.read_bytes()
146	            payload = json.loads(data)
147	            entry = _parse_entry(child, payload)
148	        except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError, InboxValidationError) as exc:
149	            _LOGGER.warning("inbox: skipping %s: %s", child.name, exc)
150	            continue
151	        entries.append(entry)
152	
153	    entries.sort(key=lambda e: (e.submitted_at, e.path.name))
154	    return entries
155	
156	
157	def pending_count(run_dir: Path) -> int:
158	    return len(scan_inbox(run_dir))
159	
160	
161	def _move_to(file_path: Path, dest_dir: Path) -> None:
162	    """Move ``file_path`` to ``dest_dir/<sha256>``.
163	
164	    The sha256-of-file-bytes filename (FLAG-P8-002) avoids any post-hoc
165	    events.jsonl re-read to derive the just-appended event hash. On OSError
166	    we unlink the original so a stuck file does not loop the inbox.
167	    """
168	    try:
169	        dest_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
170	        digest = hashlib.sha256(file_path.read_bytes()).hexdigest()
171	        target = dest_dir / digest
172	        os.replace(file_path, target)
173	    except OSError as exc:
174	        _LOGGER.warning(
175	            "inbox: failed to move %s to %s (%s); unlinking", file_path.name, dest_dir.name, exc
176	        )
177	        try:
178	            file_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
179	        except OSError:
180	            pass
181	
182	
183	def consume_inbox_entry(
184	    run_dir: Path,
185	    entry: InboxEntry,
186	    *,
187	    slug: str,
188	    projects_root: Path | None,
189	) -> bool:
190	    """Validate ``entry`` against the current cursor and consume it.
191	
192	    Returns ``True`` when an event was appended (file moved to .consumed/),
193	    ``False`` for stale or rejected entries. Stale-cursor entries are left
194	    in inbox/ so a future call can revisit them once the cursor advances;
195	    rejected entries (actor-step approve, gate failure, mismatched evidence)
196	    move to .rejected/<sha256> so they do not loop.
197	    """
198	    project_root = project_dir(slug, root=projects_root)
199	    plan_path = project_root / "plan.json"
200	    run_id = run_dir.name
201	    events_path = run_dir / "events.jsonl"
202	    consumed_dir = run_dir / INBOX_DIR_NAME / CONSUMED_DIR_NAME
203	    rejected_dir = run_dir / INBOX_DIR_NAME / REJECTED_DIR_NAME
204	
205	    plan = load_plan(plan_path)
206	    events = read_events(events_path)
207	    peek = peek_current_step(
208	        plan, events, slug, project_root=project_root, run_id=run_id
209	    )
210	
211	    if entry.decision == "abort":
212	        append_event(
213	            events_path,
214	            make_run_aborted_event(run_id, reason=f"inbox abort by {entry.submitted_by}"),
215	        )
216	        clear_active_run(slug, root=projects_root)
217	        _move_to(entry.path, consumed_dir)
218	        return True
219	
220	    # approve / retry both require the cursor to be on a matching attested step.
221	    if peek.exhausted or peek.step is None or isinstance(peek.step, CodeStep):
222	        _LOGGER.warning(
223	            "inbox: skipping %s: cursor not on an attested step", entry.path.name
224	        )
225	        return False
226	    if not isinstance(peek.step, AttestedStep):
227	        _LOGGER.warning(
228	            "inbox: skipping %s: cursor not on an attested step", entry.path.name
229	        )
230	        return False
231	
232	    cursor_step_id = peek.path_tuple[-1] if peek.path_tuple else ""
233	    if entry.step_id != cursor_step_id:
234	        _LOGGER.warning(
235	            "inbox: skipping %s: step_id %r does not match current cursor %r",
236	            entry.path.name,
237	            entry.step_id,
238	            cursor_step_id,
239	        )
240	        return False
241	
242	    if entry.decision == "approve":
243	        if peek.step.ack.kind == "actor":
244	            _LOGGER.warning(
245	                "inbox: skipping %s: ack.kind=actor not supported by inbox protocol "
246	                "(use astrid ack ...)",
247	                entry.path.name,
248	            )
249	            _move_to(entry.path, rejected_dir)
250	            return False
251	        # ack.kind == 'agent'
252	        parts: list[str] = [peek.step.command, "--agent", entry.submitted_by]
253	        for ev in entry.evidence:
254	            parts.extend(["--evidence", ev])
255	        if entry.item_id is not None:
256	            parts.extend(["--item", entry.item_id])
257	        synthesized = " ".join(shlex.quote(p) for p in parts)
258	        try:
259	            gate_command(slug, synthesized, [], root=projects_root)
260	        except TaskRunGateError as exc:
261	            _LOGGER.warning("inbox: rejecting %s: %s", entry.path.name, exc.reason)
262	            _move_to(entry.path, rejected_dir)
263	            return False
264	        _move_to(entry.path, consumed_dir)
265	        return True
266	
267	    # retry
268	    latest = _latest_event_for_path(events, peek.path_tuple)
269	    if not isinstance(latest, dict) or latest.get("kind") != "produces_check_failed":
270	        _LOGGER.warning(
271	            "inbox: skipping %s: retry requires latest event to be produces_check_failed",
272	            entry.path.name,
273	        )
274	        return False
275	    if peek.step.ack.kind != "agent":
276	        _LOGGER.warning(
277	            "inbox: skipping %s: retry only supported for ack.kind=agent",
278	            entry.path.name,
279	        )
280	        _move_to(entry.path, rejected_dir)
281	        return False
282	    args = AttestedArgs(
283	        agent=entry.submitted_by,
284	        actor=None,
285	        evidence=entry.evidence,
286	        item=entry.item_id,
287	    )
288	    try:
289	        validate_attested_identity(
290	            slug=slug, step=peek.step, args=args, run_started_actor=None
291	        )
292	    except TaskRunGateError as exc:
293	        _LOGGER.warning("inbox: rejecting %s: %s", entry.path.name, exc.reason)
294	        _move_to(entry.path, rejected_dir)
295	        return False
296	    append_event(
297	        events_path,
298	        make_cursor_rewind_event(peek.path_tuple, reason="inbox retry"),
299	    )
300	    _move_to(entry.path, consumed_dir)
301	    return True
302	
303	
304	def _latest_event_for_path(events, path_tuple: tuple[str, ...]):
305	    path_str = STEP_PATH_SEP.join(path_tuple)
306	    path_list = list(path_tuple)
307	    for ev in reversed(events):
308	        if not isinstance(ev, dict):
309	            continue
310	        if ev.get("plan_step_id") == path_str:
311	            return ev
312	        if ev.get("plan_step_path") == path_list:
313	            return ev
314	    return None
315
